4:Tabe,**Describe the bug**

The resumable local-inference downloader cannot recover when a process exits after a bundle file has been fully written to its `.part` path but before the atomic rename into the bundle directory.

On restart, the sidecar SHA still matches the current manifest, so `resumableStartByte` returns the complete file size. The downloader sends `Range: bytes=<size>-`; a compliant server answers `416 Range Not Satisfiable`. The job fails while leaving the same complete partial in place, so every retry repeats the same 416. A valid multi-GB transfer is permanently stuck until the staging file is deleted manually.

**To Reproduce**

1. Put the complete expected bytes at the bundle file staging path.
2. Put the manifest SHA256 in the adjacent `.expected` sidecar.
3. Start the bundle download against an HTTP endpoint that returns 416 for a range beginning at EOF.
4. Observe the download fail before the existing SHA gate can commit the staged file.

Fails on current `develop` (`16a038447`):

<details><summary>RED test output</summary>

```text
FAIL local inference downloader stale-content robustness > commits a complete .part after restart without requesting a range past EOF
Error: HTTP 416 from model hub for elizaos/eliza-1/text/eliza-1-2b-128k.gguf
```

</details>

**Expected behavior**

Before issuing a Range request, a SHA-pinned complete partial should be recognized by its manifest SHA, atomically moved into the bundle, and have its `.expected` sidecar removed. Incomplete matching partials must continue to use normal Range resume.

Acceptance criteria:

- A complete SHA-matching `.part` is committed without another model-file request.
- The final file is byte-identical and the staging file/sidecar are gone.
- A partial strict prefix still resumes with `Range: bytes=N-`.
- Stale or unknown-provenance partials are still discarded.
- Downloader tests, plugin checks, and repository verification pass.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - downloader persistence failure has no rendered UI surface.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- MP4/JPG: N/A - no rendered UI is involved.
- Backend logs: the exact failing consumer-boundary error is included above.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no model call, prompt, action, or provider behavior is involved.
- Domain artifacts: complete `.part` plus matching `.expected` sidecar are created by the regression test.

**Additional context**

The existing final SHA256 gate prevents corrupt installation, so this is a crash-recovery/liveness bug rather than an integrity bypass. The failure window is narrow but expensive because bundle files are multi-gigabyte and the only current recovery is manual staging cleanup.5:T11a8,## Summary

6:T103f,## Problem

Eliza currently has no canonical live lane that proves a **managed dedicated** Cloud agent can be provisioned on staging, answer a real proof-token chat turn through both bridge and SSE paths, and be cleaned up without silently exercising the shared tier.

This is a correctness/coverage bug, not a latency optimization. Several existing lanes appear to cover this path but do not:

- `hetzner-e2e.yml` is the only current workflow that combines a real proof-token `message.send` turn with raw-server teardown. It is blocked by the rejected isolated `HCLOUD_TOKEN_CI` (HTTP 401; #15616). The broader `HCLOUD_APPS_TOKEN` must not replace that scoped credential.
- `cloud-provisioning-e2e.mjs` and `live-cloud-provision-smoke.ts` create agents without `alwaysOn`/an equivalent dedicated-tier request. Current tier selection therefore defaults them to `shared`, even though their comments call the result Hetzner-backed.
- `cloud-provisioning-e2e.mjs` proves only `/api/status|health|me`; it does not send a real chat turn or delete the fresh agent.
- `live-cloud-provision-smoke.ts` has proof-token bridge/SSE checks and cleanup, but no workflow invokes it. With the current shared default, its pairing-token assertion is incompatible with the created tier. SIWE cleanup also leaves its disposable organization behind.
- `app-live-e2e.yml` wires `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY`, while the repository has `ELIZACLOUD_API_KEY`. Recent cloud-login/provision/chat jobs report green with the sole Playwright test skipped because the key is empty (for example job `86628737358`). This is green-by-skip, not live evidence.
- `launch-hardening-8756.yml` and the mobile Cloud provisioning job are status-only and do not prove dedicated chat or full cleanup. Recent mobile success reused a shared agent and completed in about 1.3 seconds.

The staging Cloud bearer itself is healthy: the latest Hetzner E2E reached and passed the Cloud auth preflight before the independent Hetzner-token 401. Staging `/api/health` also serves the expected current commit. The missing piece is truthful harness wiring.

## Why this matters

Without a fail-closed dedicated lane, shared-runtime success and skipped tests can be mistaken for proof of the Worker → dedicated router → Headscale → agent path. That leaves the final acceptance in #15347 and the dedicated leg of #16079 unverified.

## Acceptance criteria

- Add one manual/scheduled staging workflow that uses the existing managed staging provisioner and the existing repository Cloud credential; do not require or substitute the broader apps-project Hetzner token.
- Create a unique canary explicitly as dedicated (`alwaysOn: true` or the current canonical equivalent) and require the returned tier/state to be dedicated, running, database-ready, heartbeat-fresh, and mesh-addressed.
- Fail closed if the credential is missing, a test skips, the response is a canned/fabricated fallback, or the actual tier/path differs from the declared dedicated path.
- Send nonce-bearing real turns through both central bridge and dedicated SSE. Require clean completion and proof match.
- Capture privacy-safe timings/trace/path fields only; never emit credentials, prompts, model output, raw provider bodies, agent IDs, or hostnames.
- In `finally`, delete only the unique agent created by the run and clean up the disposable user/organization. Cleanup failure must fail the lane or leave an explicit reaper artifact.
- Add a no-unbounded-autoscale/cost guard or an explicitly bounded canary-capacity contract.
- Fix the App Live secret-name drift and make a zero-executed-test/skip outcome red for the declared Cloud chat job.
- Correct stale “Hetzner-backed” comments/tests so shared and dedicated coverage cannot be confused.
- Link a successful exact-deploy-SHA run back to #15347 and #16079.

**1. Mention prefix defeats command detection.** A Discord message `<@bot> /model show` (strict-mention servers require the mention) reaches the pipeline with the mention still in `content.text`, so `hasCommand()` / the pre-LLM shortcut gate (which check `startsWith("/")`) never fire. The turn falls through to the planner. Bare `/model show` text works. Fix direction: strip the leading self-mention before command detection (connector-side normalization or in the gate's text extraction).

**2. The planner then improvises — including a hallucinated success claim.** Live repro: a **non-owner** webhook user sent `<@bot> /model coding codex gpt-5.4 low`. The write path held (config untouched — verified in eliza.json), but the bot replied:

> "Switched coding model to GPT-5.4 with low reasoning effort."

A fabricated success claim for a mutation that never happened (same class as the phantom-claim rules from #7923/#7945).

**3. MODEL_SWITCH (action) has no role gate.** Earlier in the same session a guest's `<@bot> /model cloud` was planner-routed to the `MODEL_SWITCH` action, which executed a **global runtime inference switch attempt** with zero trust checks (it only failed because the cloud key happened to be unauthorized). #16171 owner-gated the slash-handler side (`/model local|cloud`), but the planner-routed action invocation path (`packages/agent/src/api/runtime-switch-routes.ts` consumers) still has no `roleGate`/`ACTION_ROLE_POLICY` floor. A global backend switch should be OWNER-gated at the action, matching the slash gate.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)8:Ta5d,## Summary

The `eliza-code-acp` first-use workspace provisioning path needs a deliberately designed cross-process build protocol. A proposed `O_EXCL` lock implementation was rejected during review and was **not** integrated because it still allowed concurrent/partial builds and broke paths containing spaces.

This is separate from the deterministic CI fix in #16167: mocked-native orchestrator tests now inject `ELIZA_ELIZAOS_ACP_COMMAND` and no longer enter real provisioning accidentally.

## Rejected implementation findings

- A hard-link/inode stale-reclaim check remained TOCTOU: two waiters could both validate stale lock A; waiter 1 could replace it with lock B; waiter 2 could then delete B using its already-authorized unlink.
- A wall-clock stale threshold could steal from a verified-live owner. `spawnSync()` may remain blocked after its timeout if the child traps `SIGTERM`, so age alone cannot prove ownership ended. PID reuse also makes liveness-only checks ambiguous.
- The mtime fast path could bypass a still-held lock as soon as a partial `dist/acp.js` appeared. A crash after touching/truncating the file could leave a permanently false-fresh artifact.
- The generated native command was a single unquoted string; `splitCommandLine()` breaks Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## Required design properties

- [ ] Deterministic multi-process exclusion with fencing or an equivalent proven primitive.
- [ ] No stealing from a verified-live owner based only on wall-clock age.
- [ ] Replacement-lock-safe cleanup; an old owner/waiter can never delete a successor's lock.
- [ ] Build to a temporary artifact, validate it, and publish atomically under the lock (or use an equivalent completion marker tied to the artifact).
- [ ] Crash/dead-owner recovery without PID-reuse ambiguity.
- [ ] One shared deadline across waiting and building, with bounded status/signal/stdout/stderr diagnostics.
- [ ] Structured `{ command, args }` propagation, or equivalently robust path handling, including workspaces/Bun binaries with spaces.

## Required tests

- [ ] Multiple processes reclaim one dead lock; exactly one fake build runs.
- [ ] A holder writes `partial` while keeping the lock; a waiter cannot return until the validated complete artifact is atomically published.
- [ ] A live owner beyond the nominal wait budget is never overlapped.
- [ ] Crash recovery and PID-reuse simulation.
- [ ] Workspace and Bun paths containing spaces.
- [ ] Failed build never leaves a fresh-looking executable artifact.

Do not solve this by increasing timeouts or reintroducing test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.
9:T8d5,## Problem

The root `test:cloud` command is not self-contained after a deterministic clean install when artifact synchronization is disabled. It begins the Cloud test batches without first making workspace runtime exports such as `@elizaos/core` available.

Other top-level lanes (`test:plugins`, `test:client`, and `test:server`) explicitly run `build:core`; `test:cloud` directly invokes `packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs`.

## Reproduction

On current `develop` in a new worktree:

```bash
ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1 bun install --frozen-lockfile
node packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs
bun run test:cloud
```

The first database/service batches fail with repeated errors shaped like:

```text
Cannot find module '@elizaos/core' from 'plugins/plugin-sql/src/index.ts'
```

This cascades into PGlite readiness failures even though the tested Cloud code is healthy.

Then:

```bash
bun run build:core
bun run test:cloud
```

The standard build completes 70/70 Turbo tasks and the same Cloud runner completes all 10 batches with zero failed tests.

## Why this matters

- A documented root test script should not depend on a previously downloaded/built artifact bundle that is absent from a clean checkout.
- `ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1` is used to prevent stale artifact bundles from overwriting checked-in fixtures/baselines during deterministic validation.
- The current failure shape looks like dozens of database regressions rather than one missing prerequisite.
- PGlite tests correctly fail loudly, so the harness should satisfy their runtime dependency rather than weakening those guards.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `bun run test:cloud` succeeds from a clean frozen install with `ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1`.
- [ ] Cloud tests resolve required workspace packages without relying on a downloaded prebuilt artifact bundle.
- [ ] Use the smallest standard prerequisite or test-runtime mechanism; do not require an unnecessary full-repo build if a narrower supported path exists.
- [ ] Preserve PGlite readiness assertions and fail-closed behavior.
- [ ] Add a CI/self-test covering the clean-install, no-artifact-sync path.
- [ ] Document any required keyword generation/build step in the command itself rather than as tribal knowledge.
a:T7b9,## Problem

`.github/workflows/chat-latency-live.yml` accepts `expected_gateway_sha` and verifies that `/api/health` serves that SHA before and after the live paired benchmark. It does not also assert that the workflow checkout's `GITHUB_SHA` equals `expected_gateway_sha`.

If `develop` advances between an exact staging deployment and workflow dispatch, the artifact can combine:

- scripts/config from a newer checkout; and
- a gateway deployment at the requested older SHA.

The served-SHA checks still pass, but the artifact is not intrinsically tied to one source revision. We can close the race externally today, but the workflow should make the invariant self-enforcing.

## Evidence

Read-only audit of the post-merge validation path for #16156 found:

- the canonical deploy guard correctly restricts staging to `develop`;
- Cloud CF Deploy stamps and verifies its exact served SHA;
- Chat Latency Live verifies `expected_gateway_sha` before and after the benchmark;
- no job step compares `github.sha` / `GITHUB_SHA` with `inputs.expected_gateway_sha`.

Historical run 29171752918 is still valid because the run/deploy provenance was checked separately. This issue hardens future artifacts.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Fail before any paid request unless `github.sha == inputs.expected_gateway_sha`.
- [ ] Keep the existing 40-character SHA validation and before/after served-health checks.
- [ ] Emit both checkout and served SHAs in the privacy-safe summary/artifact.
- [ ] Add a self-test or static workflow contract proving a mismatch fails before the benchmark command.
- [ ] Preserve canonical-ref guards; do not add a feature-branch deploy bypass.
- [ ] Document the exact merged-`develop` dispatch sequence.

## Safe interim procedure

Before dispatch, require `refs/heads/develop` to equal the served deployment SHA; immediately after dispatch, require the run's `headSha` to equal the same SHA. Stop without running the benchmark if either check fails.
b:T499,## Problem

`packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness/src/cli-run.test.ts` assigns a temporary `HOME`, but the CLI derives evidence and secret paths from `node:os.homedir()`. Under Bun, `homedir()` remained the real account home, so a test run created evidence below `/home/nubs/.moltbot/projects/eliza-fleet/evidence/voice-e2e`.

This contaminates developer or CI state and risks coupling a test to real local credentials. The two observed temporary evidence directories were removed after diagnosis.

## Acceptance criteria

- Preserve the current live default under the real user home.
- Assert every generated artifact remains below the test `mkdtemp` root on success and failure.
- Assert the real `~/.moltbot` tree has no delta after the test.
- Exercise the contract under both Node and Bun.
- Keep live-provider behavior and evidence naming unchanged outside tests.

## Evidence

The leak reproduced while running the baseline CLI evidence test during #16173 follow-up validation. Setting `process.env.HOME` was insufficient under Bun because `os.homedir()` still resolved to `/home/nubs`.c:T7a6,## Summary

`Provider.cacheStable` is part of the public `Provider` contract, but its value is never read when a provider's output is rendered into a prompt segment — so setting it has no effect.

## Where

Three spots drop the flag between the provider definition and the rendered prompt segment:

- `ContextProviderEvent` (`packages/core/src/types/context-object.ts`) has no `cacheStable` field.
- `appendStateProviderEvents` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts`) builds a `ContextProviderEvent` from `state.data.providers` but never copies `cacheStable` from the registered provider definition.
- `renderEvent`'s provider-event branch (`packages/core/src/runtime/context-renderer.ts:311`) hardcodes `stable: false`.

## Consequence

On the planner/evaluator stages, `buildStageChatMessages` buckets segments by their `stable` flag — `stable` content goes into the cached system message, `dynamic` into the uncached user message. With `stable` hardcoded `false` for every provider event, a provider that declares `cacheStable: true` still has its (genuinely stable) content routed into the uncached user message and repriced on every call.

## Why it matters

The flag is documented as functional. The plugin-starter scaffold (`packages/examples/_plugin`, the template new plugins are generated from) sets `cacheStable: true` on its example `HELLO_WORLD_PROVIDER`, and `registryPluginsProvider` sets it too. Any provider that sets it (and is rendered as a text segment rather than a native tool) is silently uncached.

Note: core's `ACTIONS`/`PROVIDERS` providers also set `cacheStable: true` but are intentionally in `MODEL_CONTEXT_PROVIDER_EXCLUSIONS` — they are rendered as native tools, not text segments — so they are correctly unaffected.

## Expected

`Provider.cacheStable` is threaded through to the rendered prompt segment's `stable` flag so `buildStageChatMessages` routes a stable provider's content into the cached system message.
d:T6ea,## Summary

`lookup.ts` now resolves independent input/output pricing reads concurrently to remove serial billing-path latency (#16156). On a fresh isolate where both sides miss the completed-value cache, both tasks can reach the same provider catalog fetch before either result is cached.

This is not a billing correctness bug: invalid/missing prices still fail closed and the returned rates are validated. It is a low-priority duplicate-I/O/cold-latency opportunity.

## Observed shape

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/lookup.ts` starts the input and output lookups concurrently.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/cache.ts` caches completed values, but does not retain an in-flight promise.
- Two same-key cold misses can therefore issue two identical external catalog reads.

## Suggested implementation

Add key-scoped single-flight caching for in-progress catalog resolutions:

- share one promise for concurrent requests for the same canonical provider/model/rate key;
- remove the promise on both fulfillment and rejection;
- keep the existing completed-value TTL cache and fail-closed validation;
- do not let a failed promise poison later retries;
- bound the in-flight map or prove its lifecycle is bounded by completion.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A deterministic test gates the provider/catalog fetch and proves two concurrent same-key cold lookups make exactly one upstream call.
- [ ] Different keys still resolve concurrently.
- [ ] A rejected in-flight fetch is observed by all waiters and a later call retries normally.
- [ ] Invalid/non-finite rates remain rejected; no fallback can under-bill.
- [ ] Typecheck, focused pricing tests, error-policy ratchet, and changed-file coverage pass.

Related: #16156.
e:T17d0,## Summary

The Cloud chat gateway still adds seconds before a Cerebras request despite the provider itself being fast. A paired staging benchmark now isolates the dominant regression: chat crosses **three Railway-TCP Redis rate-limit gates** before provider forwarding. After 30 seconds idle, the two Hono middleware gates alone add **2.45–3.11s** outside the route timer, and the handler's org gate contributes to another **0.51–0.97s** phase. Direct Cerebras remains **0.09–0.37s**.

This is not reasoning-effort overhead and it is not slow Cerebras generation. The actual gateway-to-Cerebras response-header phase was **0.09–0.33s** in every post-idle case.

| GLM / none 4096 | 3,100ms | 44ms | 965ms | 755ms | 161ms | 5,025ms |
| Gemma / omit | 2,450ms | 1,827ms | 514ms | 614ms | 89ms | 5,494ms |
| GLM / none 512 | 3,107ms | 34ms | 862ms | 789ms | 156ms | 4,948ms |

The matched direct samples returned headers/TTFT in 86–365ms.

Warm gateway p50 was still 485ms vs direct headers around 81–88ms, with gateway outliers up to 5.84s. The hidden outside-route interval reached 4.66s.

### Independent live-tail confirmation on current staging

On commit `b2e36d3`, privacy-safe unauthenticated chat probes reproduced both modes without ever calling a model provider:

- successful Railway Redis check: 556–589ms wall
- SocketRedis timeout: 2.378s, 3.250s, and 3.459s wall
- exact log: `SocketRedis operation timed out after 1000ms`
- degraded response policy: `redis-unavailable` (the limiter fell open)

Across 679 successful Worker requests in the prior 30 minutes, p95 wall time was 3.40s and p99 was 8.49s. `/api/health`, which bypasses the lazy full-app import, stayed at 0–1ms; cold chat also pays initialization for the monolithic 580-route app.

## Code-level cause

Every chat call currently stacks:

1. `bootstrap-app.ts`: global Hono IP limiter (600/min), before the chat route timer.
2. `v1/chat/completions/route.ts`: Hono API-key limiter (200/min), also before the timer.
3. `handleChatCompletionsPOST`: per-org tier limiter (60/120/300 per minute).

All three use `REDIS_URL` in staging/production. `redis-factory.ts` resolves that to `SocketRedis` over `cloudflare:sockets`, opening request-scoped TCP/TLS connections to Railway. The committed Wrangler comments simultaneously state that raw external Redis from Workers is unreliable and can hang under load. `SocketRedis` has a 1,000ms operation timeout, so the measured two outer stalls plus one inner stall match the three connection/operation failure windows. Its error cleanup awaits an unbounded `close()` sequence, so the documented 1s ceiling is not actually a wall-clock ceiling; that is consistent with 3–4.66s outside-route outliers.

The existing `X-Eliza-Preforward-Ms` header starts inside `handleChatCompletionsPOST`, so it cannot see the largest two gates and currently labels provider-header wait as `total`. This is tracked more broadly in #16079.

## Required fix

- Replace the protective global and chat route Redis gates with Cloudflare's native Rate Limiting binding. It is machine-local/non-network-blocking and designed for protective limits.
- Keep the organization-tier limit on a globally strong backend. Cloudflare's native binding is location-local and eventually consistent, so silently substituting it would weaken the purchased org quota. Batch its lease reconciliation and use one request-scoped connection or a strong Cloudflare primitive such as a Durable Object.
- Preserve explicit 429 behavior and observable policy headers/logs. Missing/misconfigured production bindings must fail visibly, not silently disable protection.
- Bound SocketRedis teardown so an operation timeout remains a real wall-clock ceiling for non-chat consumers.
- Extend correlated telemetry across global limiter, route limiter, auth, org tier lookup/limit, pool selection, balance gate/reservation, provider fetch-to-headers, first byte/token, stream completion, and deferred settlement.
- Keep production untouched until staging proves the change.

## Acceptance criteria

- Unit/integration tests cover allow, deny, binding failure/missing binding, batched lease reconciliation, and no duplicate counter increments.
- Wrangler dry-run and generated binding types pass for staging and production config.
- A real staging paired run repeats the same five Cerebras cases with at least 30 warm pairs/case plus 30-second post-idle probes, paced below limits.
- Post-idle paired excess outside the route timer (`gateway headers - route total - matched direct headers`) has p90 below 100ms, and no Redis TCP timeout appears in the two protective chat ingress limiters.
- Gateway provider-to-headers remains within 2x direct after subtracting deliberate auth/billing work; all remaining deltas are named by truthful telemetry.
- Billing reservations/deferred settlement, auth failures, 402s, and 429s retain their existing correctness semantics.

Related: #9899 (original pre-forward work, now shown incomplete for middleware), #15513 (Smart Placement), #16079 (truthful E2E telemetry), #16137 (paired live harness).
f:T107b,## Confirmed bug

elizaOS constructs a stable prompt-cache routing key, and `plugin-elizacloud` emits it, but the Cloud chat gateway silently drops it before Cerebras.

Current path:

1. `packages/core/src/runtime/provider-cache-plan.ts` builds `v5:<prefixHash>` and places it in Cerebras provider options as both `promptCacheKey` and `prompt_cache_key`.
2. `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts` resolves the key and sends both top-level forms in the native `/chat/completions` body.
3. `packages/cloud/api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts` defines a separate `ChatRequest` that contains neither field.
4. The direct pass-through `upstreamBody` copies temperature, top-p, penalties, stop, and max tokens, but not `prompt_cache_key`.
5. The AI-SDK streaming and non-streaming paths also construct no cache-key provider options.

The key therefore disappears at the Cloud gateway on both the simple pass-through path and the structured/tool-call SDK path.

## Existing implementation showing intended support

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/types.ts` already declares `OpenAIChatRequest.prompt_cache_key`.

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/vercel-ai-gateway.ts::mergeGatewayProviderOptions` already reads that field and maps it into Cerebras/eliza provider options.

The Cloud chat route’s duplicate request shape has diverged from that shared contract.

## Impact

Cerebras now accepts an optional `prompt_cache_key` routing hint. Requests sharing a key are routed to the same prompt cache on a best-effort basis, improving cache-hit rate and Time to First Token for repeated stable prefixes.

This is particularly relevant to long-context agent/planner calls and GLM-4.7. Cerebras’s current supported-model list includes GLM-4.7, GPT-OSS 120B, and Qwen 3 235B; Gemma should not be claimed as benefiting without live evidence.

Automatic prefix caching still works without the field, so this is not an outage. The bug loses an explicit affinity hint under load and makes the already-implemented client cache plan ineffective through Eliza Cloud.

## Fix

- Accept canonical `prompt_cache_key`.
- Accept `promptCacheKey` only as a backward-compatible client alias.
- Canonicalize to one provider-facing `prompt_cache_key`; do not send both forms upstream.
- Validate a nonempty string with maximum length 1024.
- Never log the raw key.
- Preserve the key through:
  - Cerebras pass-through streaming.
  - AI-SDK streaming.
  - AI-SDK non-streaming.
- Merge cache provider options without overwriting Anthropic reasoning, web-search, or other provider options.
- Keep requests without a key byte-for-byte unchanged.
- Verify that the platform Cerebras account has `prompt_cache_key` routing enabled before production rollout; if unavailable, use an explicit capability/rollout decision rather than silently dropping caller intent.

## Acceptance

- A plugin-generated `v5:<prefixHash>` reaches the mocked Cerebras fetch as exactly `prompt_cache_key`.
- Both snake-case input and camel-case compatibility input are covered.
- Pass-through and structured/tool-call SDK paths have fetch-boundary tests.
- Oversized, empty, and non-string values have explicit tested behavior.
- No raw key appears in logs, telemetry, or errors.
- Existing reasoning, tools, streaming usage, billing, and passthrough qualification tests remain green.
- Live staging proof uses two or more GLM-4.7 calls with a stable prefix/key and records:
  - `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`
  - first-token latency
  - same-key versus rotated-key behavior
- Lack of a cache hit is reported honestly and checked against account enablement, TTL, 128-token block size, and prefix stability.
- No quality or billing behavior changes; Cerebras documents cached tokens as normally priced.

## References

- Cerebras Prompt Caching:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/capabilities/prompt-caching
- Cerebras changelog, April 22, 2026:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/support/change-log
- Cerebras cache/latency metrics:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/api-reference/metrics/retrieve-metrics
- Analogous cache-plan loss fixed for OpenRouter/Anthropic: #15776 / #15777
- Related latency measurement umbrella: #15428
10:T8c1,## Problem

`computeEffectiveMaxTokens` currently applies:

`Math.max(request.max_tokens, 4096)`

to every model classified as using reasoning tokens. Both pass-through and SDK paths send that raised value upstream.

A client requesting `max_tokens:512` therefore authorizes an upstream ceiling of 4096 without any response field or header indicating that the request was changed.

The controlled Gemma/GLM latency run discovered this because direct requests used 512 while the gateway silently sent 4096, invalidating the original comparison.

## Why this is delicate

The floor was intentionally introduced by #8189/#8486 to prevent reasoning models from consuming a low budget entirely in hidden reasoning and returning an empty but billed answer. It must not simply be deleted.

Nevertheless, silently increasing an explicit caller ceiling is an API-contract and money-integrity problem: it can permit more generation, reservation, and spend than the caller requested.

## Required decision

Choose and document one safe contract:

1. Preserve an explicit caller cap; only apply a default floor when the field is absent.
2. Reject a reasoning-enabled request whose cap is too small with a clear 4xx.
3. Permit a low cap when reasoning is explicitly disabled.
4. Introduce explicit answer-vs-reasoning budget semantics.

Whichever contract is selected, expose requested and effective values in bounded telemetry and ensure billing admission uses the same effective value sent upstream.

## Acceptance

- An explicit output ceiling is never silently increased.
- Low-budget reasoning requests cannot regress to misleading `finish_reason:"stop"` with empty billed content.
- GLM-4.7 with `reasoning_effort:"none"` can use a genuinely small budget.
- Streaming and non-streaming routes behave consistently.
- Reservations and final billing are tested against the exact provider-facing budget.
- The benchmark harness records both requested and effective budgets.
- Compatibility behavior is documented.

## References

- Original broad floor: #8189
- Launch-model detection extension: #8486
- Equivalent `/v1/messages` history: #12864 / #12865
- Cerebras Chat Completions:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/api-reference/chat-completions

16:T16bb,## Summary

Overhauls the Milady chat surface across web, iOS, Android, and desktop, and lands a **native chat-transcript renderer** (SwiftUI on iOS, Views on Android, an AppKit spike on macOS) driven by one shared, versioned contract. Renamed `ContinuousChatOverlay → ChatOverlay` throughout.

Three themes:

### 1. Liquid-glass chat surface (real OS material where physics allows)
- The chat sheet floats as liquid glass: a light fill over a heavy `backdrop-filter` (blur + saturate + brightness) so the field behind glows *through* the panel, plus the directional specular rim.
- **Native glass on Capacitor**: the `GlassBridge` plugin (iOS 26 `UIGlassEffect`, Android 12+ Material) was never registered — fixed. The default ember field moves into a **native layer behind a transparent webview** (`setBackdrop`), and the inset sheet anchors a real `UIGlassEffect` panel over it (verified live via lldb: `<UIGlassEffect: …>` + an `EmberBackdropView`/`CAGradientLayer` below the `WKWebView`). The panel's corner radius tracks the sheet's animated corners.
- Maximized chat is a flat neutral `#212121` reading surface (the sheen/gradient read as artifacts full-screen); the exit is a pure cover-layer crossfade (no discrete style swap at the corners); the maximized composer capsule is glassmorphic.
- Desktop: opt-in `NSVisualEffectView` window glass behind the chat-overlay window (`ELIZA_DESKTOP_NATIVE_GLASS=1`).

### 2. Gesture correctness (the pull continuum)
- **maximize → pull-to-half** now settles at a real detent (was: bounced tall / collapsed / stranded a stub). Root causes fixed: an invisible stale grabber captured the restore drag; the restore bookkeeping raced the maximize-release hysteresis; `maximizeFromPull` never settled the height spring (stranded the GPU layer + drag latch); restore releases free-rested instead of completing.
- One **constant pointer-locked drag handle** across pill ↔ input ↔ chat (rendered outside the scale transform, y tracks the panel's visual top).
- Restore drag morphs live (shape springs home, handle fades in under the finger) instead of popping at release.
- Regression e2e: `test:chat-restore-e2e`, `test:chat-restore-continuum-e2e`.

### 3. Native chat-transcript renderer (new)
Everything in the chat renders to **native widgets** — text/markdown, code, choice, follow-ups, form (fields/select/checkbox + validation), workflow, checklist, task card, background swatches, permission card, secret request, tool-call rows, thinking disclosure, failure/retry.

- **One contract** (`eliza.native-transcript/v1`): frames serialized through the *same* `parseSegments` the DOM uses; a typed action envelope back (`message | navigate | prefill | background | retry`). A golden fixture is conformance-tested by every decoder.
- **iOS** SwiftUI, **Android** Views, **macOS** AppKit spike — all one contract.
- Security by construction: secrets are display-only (never transit chat/bridge), permission grant requires a real OS request (no fabrication), navigate/prefill/background stay local intents (never leaked to the agent as chat text).
- Harness demo: `ELIZA_NATIVE_TRANSCRIPT_DEMO=1 bun run --cwd packages/app ios:chat-harness:preview` (full-screen native, DOM composer reserved at the bottom; `eliza:native-transcript-split=1` for side-by-side review).

## Red-team

A 4-lens adversarial red-team (security / iOS correctness / DOM-parity / bridge-lifecycle) + verification judge returned **NO-GO on 5 HIGH findings**; **all fixed** and re-verified:
- iOS scroll no longer yanks a reader who scrolled up (at-bottom guard, DOM/Android parity).
- iOS widget `@State` keyed by stable segment/tool identity, not array offset (locked choice / form draft no longer adopts the wrong view's state).
- Android row fingerprint excludes streaming-volatile fields (widget drafts/locks survive token growth).
- `navigate` routes through the real `dispatchNavigateViewEvent` shape the shell reads.
- Retry is a typed `{kind:retry}` envelope gated to retryable failure kinds (no fabricated chat string).
Plus MEDs: iOS markdown link runs stripped; Android background emits shared preset ids; Android rejects non-v1 schema; Android form no longer trims; harness hides the native list on mid-`show()` unmount; sim-check fails on a native reject.

## Validation

**iOS on-simulator** — all 14 widget types rendered natively and interactive: choice (selection state), form (text input + required validation + submit producing the exact DOM-parity `[form:submit …] {json}`), permission (no grant button), secret (display-only), follow-ups, workflow (step glyphs), tool calls (running/success + duration), thinking, checklist, task, background, code, generated-UI card, failure (gated Retry). Bridge round-trip proven (native tap → `transcriptAction` → script advance → new frame → native re-render).

**Android on-emulator** — native choice + form rendered as native Views (EditText/Spinner/CheckBox/Button); bridge round-trip confirmed in logcat.

**Automated** — 558 UI vitest; native conformance: swiftc -typecheck (0 errors, full module), decode-check, 65-check widget logic script, javac (0), JVM contract 6/6; chat-sheet e2e 296/296; restore e2es green; iOS mirror byte-identical.

## Known follow-ups (non-blocking)
- Native conformance gates (Swift/JVM) are not yet wired into CI (only the TS `spec.test.ts` runs there). Add a macOS Swift lane + JVM lane on fixture/model changes.
- Deep-nesting decode depth cap (hardening; latent, native not production-wired).
- The maximized composer is still CSS glass (an in-page sheet over DOM pixels can't be lensed by an under-webview native panel); a true-native maximized input needs the dual-webview or native-composer architecture.

17:T18a2,## Realtime voice: integrated Phase-1 slice (server + client + live-provider evidence)

One vertical slice, per Definition of Done: server, browser client, and the evidence rig that proved them against **live Deepgram Flux + Cartesia Sonic**, in a single reviewable unit. Nothing here is a seam, a stub, or UI ahead of capability.

### What this is
- **Server** (`packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/**`, `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/voice-session/**`): mint route (short-lived scoped JWT, jti, aud-bound), consent-nonce precondition (server-enforced, SEC-21), WS handler with hello-first headerless auth (WebView 113 cannot set WS headers), revoke-to-silence (SEC-6: revocation severs live provider sockets), server-derived usage metering (SEC-15: client never trusted for cost), interruption pipeline under a single voiceTurnId (cancel TTS -> abort SSE -> flush downlink -> `interrupted`), uplink re-framing to Flux's 2560B/80ms frames.
- **Client** (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-*.ts`): mint -> hello-first WSS -> full event handling; mic capture with runtime-verified AudioWorklet + ScriptProcessor fallback (WebView 113); streaming PCM playback (no decodeAudioData full-clip barrier); barge-in flushes local playback before server ack; re-mint on revoked/expired token (no reconnect with a dead token); maps onto the existing unified voice status from #15924 (no second state system).
- **Evidence harness** (`packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness`): one command drives baseline / barge-in / error-auth scenarios and fails loudly if any stage or artifact is missing. `--target=real` boots the REAL server code (real jwt/consent/session/registry/metering/reframer/adapters; only the socket transport is a documented node shim in place of Workers `WebSocketPair`).

### Flag
`VOICE_REALTIME_WS_ENABLED` (default off) is consumed at the WS route: mint returns 503 `voice_realtime_disabled` when off; the evidence run exercises it ON. Real runtime consumer, not a dead flag.

### Live-provider evidence (run against the REAL server, this branch)
Evidence pack (WS transcript with tokens redacted, input/output WAVs, both-side logs, stage timings, domain rows, SHA-256 index, MP4): `evidence/voice-e2e/2026-07-10T15-37-44-748Z-real-server/` (artifacts attached below; full pack retained outside repo, hashes in the index).

| scenario | result |
| --- | --- |
| baseline (real speech in -> transcript -> LLM -> real audio out) | PASS |
| barge-in (interrupt mid-speech) | PASS, **0 post-interrupt downlink frames** |
| error-auth (bad provider key) | PASS, typed `stt_transport_error`, no ready, no audio |

Measured stage deltas (baseline, live providers, monotonic clock; missing stages report `not_reached`, never zero):
- mint -> ready: **10.5ms**
- stt_final -> llm_first_text: 834ms
- llm_first_text -> tts_first_frame: 1273ms
- **stt_final -> tts_first_frame: 2107ms** (see "Latency" below)
- transcript verbatim: "What is the weather like in Denver today?"; 1.8MB real Cartesia audio captured.

### Real bugs found ONLY by the live boot (fixed in this branch, with regression tests)
1. `end_audio` advisory frame was treated as fatal `control_unknown_type` and killed the turn.
2. Empty-transcript finalization sent Cartesia a live 400 ("No valid transcripts passed") -> tts_error, zero audio. Finalization now holds one phrase back so the terminal phrase closes the context with `continue:false`.
3. Harness `stop()` hung on half-open sockets under bun canary drain-exit.
4. Harness error-auth gate accepted only one error shape; the real adapter emits `transport_error`.

### Latency (honest)
2107ms stt_final -> first audio frame is above our 1.2s target. The measured split shows two roughly equal legs (LLM first-token 834ms, TTS first-byte 1273ms). Optimization is scoped as immediate follow-up on this same evidence rig (candidates: eager-EOT speculative LLM start, TTS warm-context reuse, first-phrase fast-path). The rig measures every run, so improvements land with numbers, not adjectives.

### Tests
124 green under bun canary (CI's bun): 31 cloud-api session, 33 cloud-shared voice-session, 60 ui client. Full WS lifecycle, barge-in zero-post-cancel, revoke <=500ms, JWT expiry/revocation/claim-mismatch, metering enforcement, hello-first, malformed/oversized frames, PCM golden vectors, playback ordering, visibility-suspend.

### Known pre-existing failure (not this branch)
`bun run verify` fails on `audit:type-safety-ratchet` on **clean develop** (dd2aac161d4) with identical feed-package violations; receipt below. This branch's surfaces typecheck clean.

### Security acceptance (from the voice threat model)
Consent nonce server-enforced before mint; revoke-to-silence severs provider sockets; metering server-derived; `mip_opt_out` note: NOT yet on develop's flux adapter (verified absent) — separate single-boundary PR follows; token never in URL or logs (redacted in evidence transcript, hand-reviewed).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Closes the staging gap where api-staging has the realtime voice server live (mint/consent/WS routes answering 200) but the app-staging bundle contains **no client path to reach it**. #16062 merged the voice-session client *library* (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-*`) but not the chat-UI wiring, so nothing in the develop-built bundle imports `createVoiceSessionClient`, and the deploy workflow never set the build-time flag.

Six commits cherry-picked from `feat/voice-standalone-backend` (the branch behind the working 2026-07-10 staging build), plus one rebase-hygiene commit:

1. `50d27a94acf` feat(ui): wire realtime voice-session client into the existing chat mic UI — `useRealtimeVoiceSession` / `useRealtimeVoiceMint` / `useContinuousVoiceSession` hooks + ChatView/chat-view-hooks integration. Realtime is an ADDITIVE enhancement of the existing continuous-mic toggle; batch path unchanged when unavailable.
2. `fbc40396a27` fix(ui): route forced realtime voice through mic controls.
3. `1065f619b3e` feat(voice): force-arm override (`VITE_VOICE_REALTIME_FORCE`, default OFF everywhere; set to "0" explicitly in the workflow).
4. `f0b9708743b` chore(cloud): enable realtime voice on staging deploy — workflow secrets plumbing + staging wrangler vars + `VITE_VOICE_REALTIME_WS` = 1 on staging, 0 on production/main.
5. `549b8c8890d` fix(cloud): staging voice auth fallback (dedicated secrets preferred, ElizaCloud API key fallback so the Worker is not deployed half-configured).
6. `c4aa4eeffa8` fix(cloud): set voice WS server `binaryType` to arraybuffer so audio uplink works.
7. `731ab66cda0` fix(ui): biome format + drop an unused `continuous` destructure that the develop rebase made dead.

19:T1b83,# Model configuration surfaces — Settings panel + /model subcommands (stage 2)

Builds the user surfaces on the stage-1 model-config API (#16164): a **Settings → Models & Providers panel** for the small / large / coding models with per-model effort dropdowns, and **`/model small|large|coding|show` slash subcommands** with catalog-backed completions. Plus two real bugs found by live-probing the result: an ungated global runtime switch, and document augmentation silently breaking every deterministic slash command in the GUI.

## What's in here

**Settings panel** (`packages/ui`, ai-model section):
- Three catalog-driven groups: Small / Large (provider → role-filtered model → per-model effort, prefilled from `GET /api/models/config` with source notes — "set by environment" etc., plus a shared-`OPENAI_REASONING_EFFORT` warning) and Coding sub-agent (codex/claude/opencode/eliza-code segmented backend, per-backend model list, codex efforts clamped to the pinned acp set, `costHint` + not-API-callable badges, default-backend switch).
- Chat saves arm an explicit **restart confirmation**, POST through a typed `updateModelsConfig` client verb (2xx/400/409 normalized into an applied/invalid/busy union), then poll status until running. Coding saves are restart-free ("applies to the next coding task").
- Typed 400s render inline with `context.supported`; 409 shows the active operation id; catalog load has distinct loading / designed-empty / error(+retry) states — a pre-stage-1 runtime yields a readable error, not a TypeError (visible in the settings-e2e screenshot below).
- All controls use the agent-addressable settings rows; `updateModelsConfig` registered in the view-action-ratchet registry.

**Slash commands** (`plugins/plugin-commands` + chat controller):
- `/model show` — current config with per-key source attribution (authorized).
- `/model small|large <model> [effort]`, `/model coding <backend> <model> [effort]` — validated writes through the same loopback route the panel uses; **owner-only**; chat writes announce the restart, coding writes say "no restart needed"; route 400s (incl. the codex-acp ultra/max pin gate) surface verbatim.
- Pre-existing `/model local|cloud` and bare per-room `/model <name>` behaviors preserved byte-identically (regression-pinned) — and `local|cloud` now carries the **same owner-only gate** (adversarial review found the global runtime switch had *no auth at all*; a connector guest could flip the inference backend).
- `dynamicChoices: "models"` in the chat composer no longer returns `[]` — completions come from the `/api/models` catalog, per-subcommand (chat providers' models vs coding backend models).

**Deterministic-command fix** (`packages/agent`) — found live, pre-existing:
- Document augmentation rewrote `message.content.text` into the contextual-documents envelope **before** the command action's `validate()` re-read it, so any slash command embedding-similar to stored docs (`/help`, `/model` on an agent with model docs) silently fell through to LLM improvisation. The pre-LLM gate matched the original text; validate saw the envelope. Command turns now skip augmentation before any retrieval work (they're instructions, not document questions). Live-proven: `/help`, `/model`, `/models` flipped from LLM-improvised to `Shortcut: cmd:*` deterministic replies.

## Evidence — live against the running agent

<details><summary>Deterministic slash battery (GUI chat route, before → after the augmentation fix)</summary>

```
BEFORE (thought field of the turn):
/help => LLM        /model show => LLM      /models => LLM      /whoami => Shortcut: cmd:whoami

AFTER:
/help       => Shortcut: cmd:help    (real command list)
/model show => Shortcut: cmd:model   ("Model configuration: **small** OPENAI_SMALL_MODEL = gemma-4-31b (config.env) ...")
/models     => Shortcut: cmd:models
/model      => Shortcut: cmd:model
```
</details>

<details><summary>Owner write + rejection through the slash path (live)</summary>

```
/model coding codex gpt-5.6-luna high
→ "Coding model for codex set to gpt-5.6-luna at high effort — applies to new coding sessions, no restart needed."
→ eliza.json: ELIZA_CODEX_MODEL_POWERFUL=gpt-5.6-luna, ELIZA_CODEX_EFFORT=high

/model coding codex gpt-5.6-terra xhigh   (restore)
→ persisted: gpt-5.6-terra xhigh

/model coding codex gpt-5.6-terra ultra
→ "Couldn't update the model config: Effort \"ultra\" is valid for gpt-5.6-terra but not parseable by the pinned codex-acp adapter (supported until the pin is bumped: low, medium, high, xhigh)"
```
</details>

<details><summary>Auth: fail-closed proven live</summary>

A non-owner (webhook user) sent `/model coding codex gpt-5.4 low` on Discord: the persisted config stayed untouched (`gpt-5.6-terra xhigh`). Note the message was *plain text* (webhooks cannot invoke native Discord slash commands); with a leading bot-mention the deterministic layer can't see the command and the planner **hallucinated a success reply** while the write path held — that planner-honesty gap + the mention-stripping gap are filed as a follow-up issue, they are not introduced here. The new `/model` subcommands also surface as real native Discord commands via the existing `catalog-commands` bridge (execute-time role gate applies).
</details>

**Settings e2e** (real-registry walkthrough, desktop+mobile): [ai-model section](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence/stage2-ai-model-section.png) — shows the new Models group rendering its **designed error state** with Retry against a harness runtime that predates the API (three-state rule; not a silent empty). [Full walkthrough video (webm)](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence/stage2-settings-walkthrough.webm). Populated-panel screenshot: N/A here — headless capture of the live authed dashboard wasn't reachable from this session; the panel's populated behavior is pinned by 16 component tests (choices filtered per model, POST payloads, 400 rendering, restart warning) and the live API proofs above; owner eyeball on the live dashboard is the remaining check.

[sol-cloud]1b:T1d70,# Model configuration API — catalog + validated config routes + spawn wiring (stage 1)

Lets an operator set the **small / large / coding sub-agent** models (and per-model reasoning effort) through one validated API, with the config actually reaching the running system: chat-brain writes restart the runtime through the operation manager; coding writes apply to the **next spawn with no restart**.

## What's in here

**`GET /api/models` → `catalog`** (additive field) — provider→model catalog with per-model effort lists, defaults, roles, and cost hints:
- **codex**: static table for gpt-5.6-sol/terra/luna, gpt-5.5/5.4/5.4-mini, spark (`apiSupported:false`), live-merged with the operator's `$CODEX_HOME/models_cache.json` (server view wins per id; corrupt/absent cache degrades to the static table — error-policy:J4)
- **claude chat/coding**: fable-5 + opus-4-8/4-7/4-6 + sonnet + haiku with per-model effort gates (xhigh/max only where the API accepts them)
- **cerebras / elizacloud**: gemma-4-31b, zai-glm-4.7, gpt-oss-120b — all three carry the `low/medium/high` `reasoning_effort` knob (**live-probed 2026-07-12**: glm modulates 90→1337 reasoning chars low→high, gemma 663→1133)

**`GET/POST /api/models/config`** — validated writes + source-attributed reads:
- POST validates target/backend/provider/model/role/effort against the catalog and writes the dual config seam (`config.env` + `config.env.vars` + `process.env`)
- chat targets request a runtime restart via the operation manager; coding targets return `restart:false`
- GET reports every key with its resolution source (`config.env` / `process.env` / `default`)
- **codex-acp pin gate**: `max`/`ultra` are valid for gpt-5.6-terra per the live catalog, but the pinned `@zed-industries/codex-acp@0.14.0` config parser cannot parse them — writing them would fail the whole `config.toml` parse and drop the model pin. The route rejects them with a typed 400 naming the pin; the bridge carries a matching two-tier guard.

**Spawn wiring (the part that makes the keys real):**
- `coding-account-bridge`: per-account `CODEX_HOME/config.toml` now resolves model as explicit `ELIZA_CODEX_MODEL` pin > app-configured `ELIZA_CODEX_MODEL_POWERFUL` > operator machine config > `gpt-5.6-terra` default, and writes a validated `model_reasoning_effort` from `ELIZA_CODEX_EFFORT`. Before this the app-configured key was **write-only** — the machine `~/.codex/config.toml` silently won every spawn (caught by the live spawn proof below).
- `acp-service.buildEnv`: claude spawns read config-env `ELIZA_CLAUDE_EFFORT` → `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` (validated) and fall back to `ELIZA_CLAUDE_MODEL_POWERFUL` → `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` when the spawn carries no explicit model. Both are config-env reads: a UI/API save applies to the next spawn without restart.
- coding defaults: codex powerful `gpt-5.6-terra` (mirrored in `CODING_MODEL_DEFAULTS`), claude powerful `claude-opus-4-8`; plugin-anthropic default bumped opus-4-7 → opus-4-8.

1d:Ta3c,## What
Two additions that let a user talk to the agent from a Discord **group DM** — a context where a bot can never read the message stream, only receive interactions:

1. **`/ask <message>`** — routes free text through the real message service and returns the agent's reply as the interaction response (chunked across `editReply` + `followUp` for >2000 chars). Works everywhere (servers, bot DMs, group DMs); degrades cleanly when the runtime/message service isn't ready or the message is empty.
2. **Opt-in group-DM / user-install commands** — `transformCommandToDiscordApi` sets `contexts: [guild, bot-DM, private-channel]` + `integrationTypes: [guild, user]` on non-guild-only commands when `DISCORD_USER_INSTALL` is enabled. Guild-only commands stay pinned to the guild context; an explicit `contexts` array still wins.

## Why off by default
Discord **rejects** user-install command registration unless the application is configured as user-installable in the developer portal. So `DISCORD_USER_INSTALL` defaults to `false` (current behavior: guild + bot-DM), and operators opt in after enabling User Install in the portal.

## Tests (10; full plugin suite 315/315)
- Transform opt-in matrix: default omits contexts; `userInstall` adds `[0,1,2]` + `[0,1]`; guild-only stays `[0]`; explicit `contexts` respected.
- `/ask` handler: routes text into the message service, DM channel-type off-guild, empty-message reject (no runtime touch), no-service graceful degrade, long-answer chunking across editReply + followUps.

1e:T103b,## What

Makes the workspace-native **eliza-code ACP** first-use provisioning path (`ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` in `plugin-agent-orchestrator`) crash-safe and multi-process safe, per the design properties in #16169.

Previously `ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` built `dist/acp.js` with a naive missing-or-stale check and no cross-process coordination:
- concurrent first spawns could interleave writes and publish a corrupt executable,
- a crash after `bun build` truncated the artifact left a partial `acp.js` whose mtime looked fresh forever,
- the launch command was an unquoted string that `splitCommandLine` broke on Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## How

New `acp-provisioning.ts` implements the deterministic protocol required by the issue. `ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` becomes a thin adapter over it, preserving the existing string-command contract (byte-identical for quote/space-free paths).

- **Fenced mutual exclusion** — lock created with `open(..., "wx")` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL)`; record carries `{pid, fence, startedAtMs}`. Ownership is the fence token, never age or PID alone.
- **No stealing from a verified-live owner** — a waiter reclaims only when the owner PID is not alive; a dead owner is reclaimed immediately (crash recovery within the outer deadline).
- **PID-reuse disambiguation** — a "live" PID older than an absolute lifetime ceiling is treated as reused and reclaimed, resolving the ambiguity the issue calls out.
- **Single-winner reclaim** — an `O_EXCL` reclaim-intent gate ensures exactly one reclaimer replaces a stale lock and runs the build at a time (the shared `bun run build` canonical output is not safe to run concurrently). The intent records its holder PID so a crashed holder is cleared immediately, not after the ceiling.
- **Gap-free lock replacement** — reclaim installs the new lock via a temp+atomic-`rename`, so `paths.lock` is never momentarily absent (no window for a third `open("wx")` acquirer).
- **Atomic publish + completion marker** — build writes to a private per-fence temp, is validated (non-empty, marker present), then `rename`d into place; a `dist/.acp.done` marker records the published size+mtime. Freshness is keyed on the marker, so a bare/partial `acp.js` is never treated as fresh and a failed/invalid build never leaves a fresh-looking artifact.
- **Space/quote-safe command** — `formatAcpCommand` quotes only when needed and picks the quote char the path lacks, so paths with spaces or quotes round-trip through `splitCommandLine` (which has no escape grammar). Quote-free paths stay bare (unchanged behavior).

## Tests

`acp-provisioning.test.ts` (24 tests) simulates crash-at-each-step via injectable `build` / `isPidAlive` / `now` hooks and covers every required scenario:
- multiple processes reclaim one dead lock, exactly one build runs;
- a partial/unmarked artifact is never fresh; a failed/invalid build leaves no fresh artifact;
- a verified-live owner is never overlapped; crash + PID-reuse recovery;
- malformed-lock recovery (crash mid-claim) with a mid-claim grace window;
- single-winner reclaim: a held gate blocks a second reclaimer; a dead-holder gate is cleared immediately; an aged-out gate is recovered;
- gap-free lock install; overlapping builds never corrupt/partially publish (atomic-publish invariant);
- space- and quote-containing paths round-trip through the command.

**Evidence (VPS, bun canary):**
- `acp-provisioning.test.ts` + `eliza-code-acp-install.test.ts` + existing `acp-service.test.ts`: **82 passed**.
- Coverage on `acp-provisioning.ts`: **~85% lines / ~80% branch / 95% funcs** (>= 50% bar).
- `tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors. Biome: clean. `error-policy-ratchet` vs `origin/develop`: `emptyCatch 0->0`, no new fallback-slop.
- Codex review (`gpt-5.5`) against the commit: no findings after iterating through the P1/P2 races it surfaced (successor race, PID reuse, shared canonical build output, intent-timeout-vs-deadline, error-policy annotations) — all fixed in this branch.

Does not increase timeouts and does not reintroduce test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.

Closes #16169

20:T2408,Fixes #15901, #15902, #15903 — the three onboarding defects isolated on a physical Pixel 6a during the #14358 Android close-out (evidence + logcat timelines: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/14358#issuecomment-4930960082). They share the client handoff surface and are fixed coherently here. **Do not merge without review — reviewer sign-off requested.**

## Root causes and fixes

### #15901 — handoff hard-fails during the proxy-route 404 window
**Root cause.** Control-plane `running` precedes actual routability: the runtime proxy can 404 the dedicated subdomain for minutes after the record flips (structural window, see the CP-side findings annotated on #15901 — the router only routes once `web_ui_port` + mesh IP register, and recovery is reactive). The client treated `status==="running" && url` as ready and did a **one-shot** import; the thrown 404 landed `phase: failed` seconds into a 10-minute budget → "Setup paused / Retry" for an agent that was actually coming up.
**Fix.**
- `resolveReadyBase` (`packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts`) now probes the dedicated base itself (`GET /api/health`) and only reports ready once the proxy actually routes — 404/408/425/429/5xx or a network throw means "running but not yet routable", so the readiness poll keeps going inside its budget and the UI stays honestly in "Setting up…".
- `runConversationHandoff` (`packages/ui/src/cloud/handoff/conversation-handoff.ts`) retries transient step failures (`HandoffTransientError`: retryable HTTP statuses + network-layer throws, classified in `cloud-handoff-supervisor.ts`) by re-entering the readiness loop under **one shared deadline** — a step failure never re-arms a fresh budget, non-transient errors (401/403/…) still fail immediately, and an exhausted budget reports `timed-out`/`failed` honestly (user stays on the working shared adapter, Retry stays armed).
- CP-side "expose routable on the detail DTO" was investigated and deliberately not bundled (spans router daemon + CF Worker + SDK mirror); full findings + file map annotated on #15901.

### #15902 — stale `eliza:cloud-handoff-pending` marker pins "Setting up… / shared" forever
**Root cause.** The home provisioning tile rendered "Setting up…" for ANY shared-adapter session, forever, with nothing pending; a leftover marker from a previous (failed) onboarding also suppressed the fresh upgrade path in `bindCloudAgent`, and the marker was only reconciled on the boot-poll 404 path.
**Fix.**
- The widget (`agent-provisioning.tsx`) tracks a shared-adapter session only while a pending-handoff marker **for that agent** exists (the marker load TTL-clears expired entries); no marker + no live phase → render real state (nothing). A phase-event cache (`events/index.ts` + `useCloudHandoffPhase`) covers late mounts so an in-flight `migrating` is still visible.
- `bindCloudAgent` (`first-run-finish.ts`) clears a mismatched leftover marker, resumes a matching interrupted migration **at the landing** (`resumePendingCloudHandoff`, previously only reachable via the boot-poll 404 path), and never mints a second dedicated target for a reused agent that already owns one (`bridgeUrl` set).
- The dedicated-base boot landings in `startup-phase-poll.ts` also reconcile (a marker is provably stale once the active server is off the shared base).
- The Android spec's `resetKeys` now include `eliza:cloud-handoff-pending`.

### #15903 — `eliza:first-run-complete` never persisted on the returning-account landing
**Root cause.** Durable completion was persisted only through the conductor's React completion-callback chain; landing paths that bypass or outrun it (returning-account reuse, the pre-navigate pairing handoff that unloads the page) left the flag unset, so the next launch could re-enter first-run for a healthy agent.
**Fix.** Every successful cloud landing persists the flag **headlessly at the landing itself** in `bindCloudAgent` — fresh provision, returning-account reuse, in-process pairing, and before the `navigate` pairing hand-off — unified with (and idempotent to) the conductor's own `setFirstRunComplete(true)` persist.

## Tests

- `conversation-handoff.test.ts` — transient-retry within budget, readiness re-poll between retries, non-transient immediate fail, budget exhaustion honesty, single shared deadline, classification tables.
- `cloud-handoff-supervisor.test.ts` — 404-window import survival, network-throw-as-transient, non-retryable 401 fails once.
- `client-cloud-handoff-target.test.ts` — proxy-readiness gate: not ready while `running` 404s, 401-is-routable, honest timeout.
- `client-cloud-connect-mock-cloud.test.ts` — **mock-stack e2e (real HTTP mock cloud) with delayed proxy readiness**: the router 404s a `running` agent for N calls; the real `startCloudAgentHandoff` waits it out, imports, switches, and the dedicated store holds the copied transcript; plus a never-routes lane asserting `timed-out` (never failed-fast).
- `pending-handoff-store.test.ts` (new) — TTL expiry clears on read, malformed/missing-field self-clean, roundtrip.
- `agent-provisioning.test.tsx` — marker-gated tile: renders with a matching marker, hides with none (the #15902 pin), hides on a mismatched marker.
- `first-run-finish.reused-shared-handoff.test.ts` — full gate contract: fresh fires, reuse-no-marker fires, matching marker → resume-at-landing, mismatched marker → cleared + fresh handoff, bridged agent never re-mints, **flag persisted on fresh + reuse landings**.

- [ ] After merge, validate the exact automatically deployed `develop` merge SHA with the same 320-record paired harness before any production promotion.

# Risk

**Medium.** This changes protective counters on every Cloud API/chat request and hardens a shared Redis socket client. Missing or throwing production rate-limit bindings fail visibly with 503. Cloudflare counters are deliberately used only for the global flood backstop and chat API-key gate; exact organization/purchased-quota accounting remains Redis-backed and authoritative.

# Background

## Root cause

Paired staging/direct evidence showed Cerebras responding in roughly 89–333 ms while the Cloud gateway could spend 2.45–3.11 seconds before the handler after idle. Privacy-safe unauthenticated probes reproduced two sequential Railway TCP Redis checks at 2.38–3.46 seconds without calling a model.

The two outer protective limiters were paying cross-cloud TCP setup before every chat turn. Independent review also found that a timed-out SocketRedis operation could resume after its 50 ms teardown bound and touch a successor connection's mutable reader/parser.

## What this PR changes

- moves the global 600/min flood backstop and chat 200/min gate to Cloudflare Rate Limiting bindings;
- preserves the exact organization inference limiter on Redis;
- hashes API-key/anonymous identifiers before using them as counter keys;
- batches carried Redis lease increments and TTL lookup into one pipeline exchange;
- makes SocketRedis reader/writer/parser state connection-local and protects publication with an ownership epoch;
- bounds teardown while ensuring timeout losers cannot read from, publish over, or close successor connections;
- starts independent input/output pricing reads concurrently and evicts the short-lived balance hint on central credit mutations;
- preserves Cerebras `promptCacheKey` when `reasoningEffort` also contributes provider options, with streaming and non-streaming regressions;
- preserves the inner 200/min chat limiter's headers while Hono unwinds through the outer 600/min limiter, for both allowed and denied responses; and
- repairs/restores process-global Redis mocks used by the complete Cloud lane.

It does **not** change provider/model selection, billing rates, response streaming bytes, database schema, or production deployment state. Native rate-limit metadata is intentionally more truthful than before.

## Reviewer entry points

1. `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/middleware/rate-limit-hono-cloudflare.ts`
2. `packages/cloud/api/src/bootstrap-app.ts`
3. `packages/cloud/api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts`
4. `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/cache/socket-redis.ts`
5. `packages/cloud/api/wrangler.toml`

# Known limits and follow-ups

- Cold startup is not fixed: two of five cold gateway cases remained about 5.2 seconds, with roughly 3.2 seconds in auth. The monolithic 16 MiB/646-route Worker bootstrap is #16155; cold auth reconnect investigation is #16159.
- One Gemma response had 121 ms preforward and 181 ms response headers, then 2.36 seconds from headers to first token. That tail is downstream/provider/stream scheduling, not ingress.
- `X-Eliza-Preforward-Ms total` currently freezes after the streaming handler returns a `Response`, so only named auth/mid/reserve components are individually attributable. Truthful correlated hop telemetry remains #16079/#16098.
- Parallel cold pricing misses can duplicate a provider catalog fetch because completed values, but not in-flight promises, are cached. Billing remains fail-closed/correct; promise-level single-flight is #16162.
- Chat Latency Live verifies the served SHA before/after but does not yet bind its checkout SHA internally; #16185 tracks that provenance hardening. The post-merge run will enforce both SHAs externally.
- The five cold and five post-idle samples are directional; warm conclusions are supported by 150 pairs.

22:Tfb0,The multi-account operator surface, requested for Discord/GUI/Telegram parity with the Settings account UI. Built in the plugin-commands catalog (reaches every connector through the existing bridges) following the `/model` loopback-route precedent.

## `/accounts` — manage the coding-account pool
- **bare `/accounts`** — per-provider report: strategy + each account's label, email, enabled/disabled, priority, session%/weekly% usage, reset countdown, health. Only providers that have linked accounts.
- **`/accounts use <provider> <account>`** — make an account active: enables it and disables its enabled siblings within that provider (scoped — no cross-provider bleed), then re-reads and lists the resulting states.
- **`/accounts enable|disable <provider> <account>`** — single toggle.
- **`/accounts strategy <provider> <priority|round-robin|least-used|quota-aware>`** — set the rotation strategy.
- **`/accounts refresh <provider> [account]`** — force a usage re-probe, then show fresh numbers.
- Provider wire ids **and** aliases (`claude`→anthropic-subscription, `codex`→openai-codex, `cerebras`→cerebras-api). Account matching by exact id, ≥6-char id prefix, exact label, or exact email — case-insensitive; **ambiguous or no match refuses with the candidate list, never guesses.**

## `/backend` — default coding backend
- **bare `/backend`** — the effective `ELIZA_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE` + the available backends.
- **`/backend codex|claude|opencode|eliza-code`** — persists through a new additive `defaultBackend`-only shape of `POST /api/models/config` (dual seam config.env + config.env.vars + process.env, restart-free, `eliza-code`→`elizaos` wire spelling). **Never `runtime.setSetting`** — that's in-memory only and reverts on restart.

## Auth
Both `requiresAuth` (reads authorized-only) + in-handler `isElevated` re-check on every write — matching `/model`. Adversarial review caught an earlier `requiresElevated`-at-definition version that made the connector bridge refuse the bare read before the handler ran; fixed and pinned with a gate-layer test.

## Root causes

- Clean runners installed with `--ignore-scripts` but built a hand-maintained subset of the scenario runner's transitive workspace graph, exposing missing dist artifacts one package at a time.
- `wake-up-routine` was classified as a `simple` wellness reply at Stage 1, so explicit first-person sleep/recovery telemetry never reached `OWNER_HEALTH`.
- `app-list` selected `APP action=list`, but the live harness had no real agent API host behind the production loopback client. The tool returned `fetch failed`; retries then consumed the 120-second turn.

## Fix

- Build the complete Turbo `@elizaos/scenario-runner...` dependency closure, run all live roots with workspace source conditions, and remove build-time model secrets.
- Declare the agent server's dynamically imported `@elizaos/plugin-app-manager` dependency so Turbo includes it.
- Add a health-owned Stage-1 evaluator that routes explicit owner telemetry reads to `OWNER_HEALTH`, while excluding clinical-advice requests and staying inactive when the host action is absent.
- Start the real production agent API server on an ephemeral port in the app-list live scenario. No fetch shim or timeout increase. Assert successful structured installed/running arrays.
- Strengthen both scenarios with successful-action and selected-argument checks.

25:T10aa,## Summary

- preserve every explicit `max_tokens` value as the provider-facing output/spend ceiling, including reasoning and Anthropic extended-thinking models
- retain the 4096 reasoning safety default only when callers omit `max_tokens`
- classify empty-but-billed results as `finish_reason: "length"` consistently in streaming and non-streaming responses
- add regression coverage for named, catalog-detected, and Anthropic reasoning paths
- rebase onto `origin/develop` (Cerebras `reasoning_effort` support, #16087) and fix a `mock.module` leak in `chat-completions-explicit-max-tokens-route.test.ts` that crashed the `test:cloud` batch containing it and `chat-completions-tool-choice.test.ts`

## Reproduction

Before this change, `computeEffectiveMaxTokens(10, null, "minimax/minimax-m3")` returned `4096`, and an Anthropic request with `max_tokens: 1000` plus an 8000-token thinking budget returned `12096`. The gateway therefore authorized more provider output and spend than the caller requested.

Separately, `bun run test:cloud`'s batching runs `chat-completions-explicit-max-tokens-route.test.ts` and `chat-completions-tool-choice.test.ts` in the same bun process. The former's top-level `mock.module()` calls narrowly overrode `modelUsesReasoningTokens` and `canonicalizeCerebrasModelId` instead of relying on the spread real exports, and bun's `mock.module` restore does not propagate to `route.ts`'s already-bound imports when both files load in one process — so the narrow mocks leaked into the sibling file's Cerebras reasoning assertions and crashed the batch.

## Verification

- `bun test packages/cloud/api/__tests__/chat-completions-explicit-max-tokens-route.test.ts packages/cloud/api/__tests__/chat-completions-tool-choice.test.ts` (both orders) — **0 fail, 64 tests**
- `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/api typecheck` — clean (`tsgo --noEmit` + worker-bundle dry-run)
- `bun run test:cloud` (full local batch run, 10 batches) — batch 10/10 fails on `Cannot find module '@elizaos/plugin-sql'` in `packages/cloud/services/agent-server` webhook/infra/script tests unrelated to this change (pre-existing worktree module-resolution gap, not touched by this PR); the batch containing the two files this PR modifies passed cleanly.

27:Tc64,## Summary

Wires the merged Cartesia Sonic 3.5 adapter (#15949) into `/api/v1/voice/tts` as the synthesis engine for **WAV-format requests** (codec-less clients — e.g. the Light Phone III's LightOS WebView, which has no MP3 decoder). Buffered ElevenLabs PCM took ~3s per reply and WAV requests can never use the MP3-shaped first-line cache; Cartesia streams raw `pcm_s16le` in well under a second.

- `cartesia-synthesis.ts` — drives `CartesiaSonicTtsAdapter` over a Cloudflare-Workers outbound WebSocket (fetch-upgrade + `accept()`, wrapped to satisfy the adapter's synchronous factory contract and its queue-until-open behavior), buffers frames, wraps in a WAV header. Throws on provider error / silent socket.
- Route: WAV requests use Cartesia when `CARTESIA_API_KEY` is set **and the caller did not pin a voice identity** (custom voices and explicit non-proxy-default ElevenLabs ids keep ElevenLabs). Any Cartesia failure falls back to ElevenLabs — voice never drops.
- Default MP3 path, first-line caches, Kokoro branch, custom voices: **unchanged**.

## Billing (money-safe by construction)

The reserve → `billFlatUsage` flow is untouched and always charges the **ElevenLabs catalog rate** — a Cartesia-served request costs the user exactly what the same request cost yesterday (Cartesia's upstream cost is lower, not higher, so this never under-bills). Usage records attribute the true engine (`provider=cartesia`, `model=sonic-3.5`, `metadata.synthesisEngine`) while `metadata.billingSource` stays `elevenlabs` (the catalog the totals came from). A dedicated Cartesia catalog entry (and a retail price decision) can follow separately.

Adds `CARTESIA_API_KEY` / `CARTESIA_DEFAULT_VOICE_ID` to the Worker bindings.

## Tests

- Unit (injected in-memory socket, real adapter): frames→valid WAV, provider-error throw, no-audio throw — `__tests__/cartesia-synthesis.test.ts` (3 pass).
- All existing `v1/voice/tts` suites pass (25 tests / 4 files).
- Typecheck + biome clean.

28:T1235,## Summary
- derive dedicated-agent ingress from the active Cloud environment instead of hardcoding production
- preserve the control plane's authoritative execution tier so dedicated onboarding never binds a shared-runtime bridge as a dedicated agent
- force a fresh dedicated create when an account only has shared agents, while keeping shared mode on the shared adapter
- repair legacy persisted cloud targets in a bounded background reconciliation without delaying startup
- reconcile the environment-specific agent domain and protected node SSH key into the provisioning control plane on deploy
- remove the duplicate Cartesia environment declaration that blocked the touched UI typecheck

## Root cause
Three independent assumptions combined into the staging failure:

1. The compatibility adapter discarded `executionTier`, so the UI inferred placement from URLs and could treat a shared-runtime bridge as a dedicated sandbox.
2. Dedicated URL fallback always used `<agent>.elizacloud.ai`, even when the active control plane was staging.
3. Staging's router domain and provisioning-node SSH credential had drifted from the protected deployment environment, producing unroutable agents and failed dedicated provisioning.

The fix keeps placement authoritative, scopes URL construction to the active Cloud environment, repairs only stale persisted targets, and makes the deployment reconcile the required runtime configuration.
